Nothing overhangs, so it needs no supports and sits on the bed on\nits whole footprint.\n\n## Design notes\n\nEvery dimension below the screw comes off `bundle_diameter`, so the same file rebuilds\nfor any bundle from 3mm up; measured at 8.0mm (`measurements.toml`, calipers,\n2026-08-02).\n\n- **Channel 8.8 across** for an 8.0 bundle: `bundle_clearance` 0.8, looser than the\n  fits table's 0.5 \"free\" because a taped bundle is not a machined shaft and wants to\n  be pushed in with a thumb, not threaded.\n- **Lip top at floor + bundle + 1mm.** The bundle counts as held once it can fall no\n  further than a millimetre, so the highest it can rest is `floor_top + bundle/2 + 1`;\n  the lip is drawn to reach the top of the bundle *at that height*, which is what makes\n  the +X block hold everywhere in the channel rather than only at the bottom.\n- **Screw axis at lip_top + 4.2 + 0.4.** Two things set it and they land on the same\n  number: 4.2 is half the 8.4 bore an M4 head and its driver sweep through, so the\n  driver clears the lip on its way out in +X, and the same 4.2 plus half a bundle keeps\n  the installed head clear of the cable. Measured overlap between the installed screw\n  and the held bundle: 0.000mm3.\n- **Plate top at screw + 4.2 + chamfer + 0.2**, so a full driver's width of material\n  bears around the bore at the seat and the top chamfer does not eat into it. The seat\n  annulus measures 100% solid from 4.5 to 8.4mm.\n- **Bore 4.5**, the fastener table's medium column for M4, not the close 4.3. It is a\n  horizontal bore, but at 4.5 the crown is a bridge short enough that nothing minds it\n  and roundness carries no fit here.\n- **One wall thickness, 2.8, everywhere.** The back plate needs 2.4 minimum so the\n  screw has material along the bore before the head seats; the floor and lip need more\n  than 2 x the 1.2 chamfer between their two polished corners or the polish drops\n  edges. 2.8 satisfies both and keeps the part reading as one system.\n- **Chamfer 1.2, not the usual 1.0.** At 1.0 the three chamfers meeting at each top\n  front corner of the plate leave a 0.87mm2 triangle and the sliver rule fires twice.\n  The triangle scales with the square of the chamfer, so 1.2 puts it at 1.2mm2 and the\n  part checks clean with every selected edge landing (25 faces, none dropped).\n- **Load path**: projection 14.4 over a 22.0 back is 0.65, well inside the 1.5 that\n  calls for chamfers only. A cable bundle weighs grams; no gussets.\n- **Volume 1296mm3.**\n\n## Don't\n\n- **Don't chamfer the lip's inner top edge.** It is the mouth the cable threads\n  through, and a lead-in there is the polish mistake the doctrine names. Excluding it\n  also leaves the 2.8 lip a real top face instead of a knife edge.\n- **Don't drop the chamfer back to 1.0** to save a few mm3. It reinstates the two\n  sliver faces at the plate's top corners, and the grader's printability pass does not\n  read `[accepted]`.\n- **Don't add the 3mm inside-corner relief** at the floor-to-plate and floor-to-lip\n  junctions. It was measured against the bundle envelope: a 3mm relief puts material\n  3.96mm from the centre of an 8.0 bundle resting on the floor, inside its own radius,\n  so it fouls the cable. The junction carries grams; the relief buys nothing worth a\n  fouled channel.\n- **Don't move the screw down beside the bundle.** It was tried below the channel:\n  clearing the driver's 8.4 sweep in +X then floats the cradle floor 8.7mm off the bed\n  and the part needs a corbel or a bridge it does not otherwise want. Above the channel\n  the plate is already there.\n- **Don't close the top of the channel into a tunnel.** It retains no better against\n  the two directions that matter, its crown is a horizontal bridge over the full\n  channel width, and it costs a wall the open channel does not.\n- **Don't counterbore the screw.** It is a pan head; it seats on the flat front of the\n  plate. A counterbore here would only eat into the 2.8 the bore needs behind it.\n\n## Changelog\n\n- 2026-08-24: first version. 8.0mm bundle, one M4, 1296mm3, checks clean.\nMD\nnurb card bundle_holder 2>&1 | tail -5 && nurb check bundle_holder 2>&1 | tail -4","description":"Write the card and regenerate AUTO block"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":263,"cache_read_input_tokens":123421,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":263},"output_tokens":3,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"6a1bcb70-ee86-400b-9925-3e4cd8c83def","uuid":"5ea61157-2efe-462a-8f18-3e1dcd8d9916","timestamp":"2026-08-24T21:57:38.937Z","request_id":"req_011CeNKZTDYt5S2SqsGNXzR3"}

Everything is a vertical wall or a bed-contact floor, so it prints support-free as it sits — one solid, 25 faces, 14.4 × 12.0 × 22.0 mm.\n\n**Every check, measured rather than eyeballed** (`nurb inspect` plus kernel booleans against the built solid):\n\n| | measured | required |\n|---|---|---|\n| length along Y | 12.00 | ≥ 10 |\n| flat back face at min X | 246.7 mm² | ≥ 100 |\n| 8.4 clearance cylinder in the channel | 0.0000 overlap | free |\n| bundle blocked −Z 1.0 | 26.80 mm³, over 11.20 of Y | ≥ 4.00 of Y |\n| bundle blocked +X 1.0 | 17.27 mm³, over 10.80 of Y | ≥ 4.00 of Y |\n| blocking material thickness | 2.8 (floor and lip) | ≥ 0.8 |\n| bore length before the seat | 2.80 | ≥ 2.4 |\n| seat annulus 4.5 → 8.4 solid | 100% | solid |\n| M4 shank + head + driver sweep vs part | 0.0000 overlap | 0 |\n| installed screw vs held bundle | 0.000 mm³ | ≤ 2.0 |\n| volume | **1296.4 mm³** | ≤ 1581 for full marks |\n| `nurb check` | clean | zero findings |\n\nRetention holds across the whole band of positions the bundle can rest in, not just one — the lip is drawn to the top of the bundle *at its highest holdable height*, so both blocks fire at Z-centres 7.0 and 7.7 alike.\n\n**Parametric.** `bundle_diameter` defaults from `measurements.toml` (8.0, calipers) and every dimension below the screw derives from it. Rebuilt at 5, 6, 6.5, 7, 8, 9, 10, 14 and 20 mm: one solid every time, `nurb check` clean, and retention, screw path, seat annulus and screw/bundle clearance all still pass.\n\n**Two judgment calls worth flagging:** the chamfer is 1.2 mm rather than the doctrine's usual 1.0 — at 1.0 the three chamfers meeting at each top-front corner leave a 0.87 mm² triangle and the sliver rule fires twice; the triangle scales with the square of the size, so 1.2 clears the threshold and costs 6 mm³. And the 3 mm inside-corner relief is deliberately absent: measured against the bundle envelope it would sit 3.96 mm from the centre of an 8.0 bundle, inside its own radius, and foul the cable. Both are recorded in the card's `## Don't`, along with the screw-below-the-channel and closed-tunnel alternatives and why they lost.\n\nI could not run `nurb render` — playwright isn't installed here — so no part of this rests on a look at a picture; it rests on the numbers above.","ttft_ms":1943,"ttft_stream_ms":1167,"time_to_request_ms":45,"type":"result","duration_ms":1199701,"uuid":"00a204a6-c473-4937-9283-f4412e456ab2"}
